check-model-standalone.mjs

self-contained validator: checker.js, fixer.js and both runners in one file — no network needed at runtime

Fetch the file itself if you can — https://www.appwithai.org/guide/check-model-standalone.mjs — and ignore this page. It exists for one case: a fetch layer that reads pages but refuses application/javascript, or a code sandbox with no outbound network where a browse tool can read pages but cannot transfer a module file into the execution environment.

bytes 202051
sha256 3d2c160fe9370aaab97194cf9d15c805e70a39b01b9d0a71fc4e70d99a8835fc

The block below is that file, base64-encoded. Base64 carries no HTML-special character and ignores whitespace, so reflowing, wrapping or converting this page to Markdown cannot corrupt it. Reconstruct and verify:

base64 -d < check-model-standalone.mjs.b64 > check-model-standalone.mjs
sha256sum check-model-standalone.mjs      # must print 3d2c160fe9370aaab97194cf9d15c805e70a39b01b9d0a71fc4e70d99a8835fc

Or in Node, with the block as a string:

writeFileSync("check-model-standalone.mjs", Buffer.from(b64.replace(/\s+/g, ""), "base64"));

Once the file is on disk, run it directly — it carries checker.js, fixer.js and both runners compressed inside it and needs no other files:

node check-model-standalone.mjs <model>.mmd
node check-model-standalone.mjs <model>.mmd --audit

Exit codes: 0 = OK, 1 = errors or warnings, 2 = errors only. The verdict line is OK — N errors, N warnings (checker) or the audit checklist score (audit). Both counts are reportable as official checker results.

See also: source index · browser validator
